### plan-r5.md

# Plan: f-20260910-09 — import replaces its owning New Tab through a fresh-id transaction

## Goal

Close `f-20260910-09`. Importing a game from the New Tab page (file, Link or FEN form of
`ImportModal`) must never write the tree of an existing tab id before its workspace metadata is
committed, must never overwrite a tab other than the one that asked, must keep the prior durable,
pending and live-cached state intact when any write is refused, and must be retryable.

MANDATE (fixed, verbatim from the ledger):

> importing a selected file into the current tab seeds the existing tab id before the workspace
> metadata is saved. A quota rejection of the workspace write leaves the prior durable origin/name
> referencing the replacement tree. A pending old tree also takes precedence over this seed in
> TabStorageRepository.read and can overwrite it on flush. The live cached store is not replaced
> by seed. … Open question: What shared existing-tab replacement transaction updates live store,
> pending tree, durable tree and metadata together while retaining the original game on failed
> commit and handling async import ownership? … the Link and FEN branches in ImportModal.tsx also
> seed the existing tab ID inside currentTabAtom updater callbacks, so the same existing-owner
> replacement design must cover all three import forms. Proof required: real-store tests with
> pending and cached old tree, successful replacement, quota failures on tree and workspace
> writes, stale async result, reload and retry; UI import journey through the container harness.

## Located facts (BASE 1854e32c)

* `src/components/tabs/ImportModal.tsx:112-124, 173-183, 193-205` — all three forms call
  `tabStorage.seed(prev.value, tree)` inside a `currentTabAtom` updater, then return new tab
  metadata. `seed` writes sessionStorage synchronously (`src/state/store/tabStorage.ts:458-474`)
  before `commitWorkspaceAtom` (`src/state/atoms.ts:76-103`) attempts the envelope write; a refused
  envelope leaves the old metadata over the new durable tree. `readTree` prefers `pending`
  (`tabStorage.ts:362-367`), and a cached zustand store (`src/state/store/tree.ts:197-201`, reused
  by `TreeStateProvider` and by `BoardsPage.closeTab` at `BoardsPage.tsx:106`) keeps the old state
  and rewrites it through `persist` on its next change.
* `ImportModal` is rendered only by `NewTabHome` (`NewTabHome.tsx:233`), which knows its own tab id
  (`id` prop, `BoardsPage.tsx:450`). `BoardsPage` renders panels with `keepMounted={false}`
  (`BoardsPage.tsx:268`), so after an `await` in `handleSubmit` the active tab — the one
  `currentTabAtom` resolves — can be any other tab the user switched to, including an analysis tab
  with a real game. The Enter handlers (`ImportModal.tsx:291, 303`) call `handleSubmit` even while
  `loading`, so two imports can race.
* `setCurrentTab`'s boolean result is ignored; `addRecentFileAtom` runs even when the commit was
  refused (`ImportModal.tsx:126-132`).
* The new-id protocol from `d-20260910-10` already exists: `commitNewTab` (`src/utils/tabs.ts:57-104`)
  seeds a fresh id, commits tabs and activation in one envelope write, and on seed failure or
  refusal rolls back only the fresh id (`rollbackCreatedTree`, `tabs.ts:106-111`, with
  `recordFailedAdmission` replayed at startup). It already replaces a lone `"new"` tab in place.
  `commitWorkspaceAtom` records every id dropped from `tabs` as a durable pending-removal intent
  (`atoms.ts:78-97`, `workspace.ts:142-160`). `closeWorkspaceTabAtom` (`atoms.ts:132-160`) then
  reclaims tab-local state: `removeFileFreshness`, `tabStorage.remove` (pending + durable),
  `disposeTabAtoms`; `BoardsPage` additionally calls `closeTreeStore` (`BoardsPage.tsx:146`).
* Import cycle constraint: `src/utils/files.ts` imports `@/state/atoms`, and `src/utils/tabs.ts`
  imports `./files`, so `src/state/atoms.ts` must not import `@/utils/tabs`.

## Approach

The answer to the open question is **not to replace an existing id at all**. The owning tab is a
`"new"` tab; the import becomes a fresh tab id that takes the owner's place in the tab list in the
same single envelope write. This is the `d-20260910-10` new-id protocol applied to an in-place
position, so the owner's durable tree, pending tree and cached store are never written before the
commit and are simply retained on any failure. Only after a successful commit is the owner id
reclaimed exactly as a closed tab is.

### O1 — Owner binding

`ImportModal` takes the owning tab id as a prop (`NewTabHome` passes its `id`), captures it when a
submit starts, and never resolves the target through `currentTabAtom`. `ImportModal` no longer
imports `currentTabAtom` or calls `tabStorage.seed`.

### O2 — Shared staged admission and the replace-new-tab transaction (depends on nothing)

**Shared staged admission (rule 11).** `commitNewTab` (`src/utils/tabs.ts:57-104`) already
allocates a fresh id, seeds it, commits, and rolls back. Extract that sequence into one helper in
`src/utils/tabs.ts` that takes the tree to seed (optional, as today), the existing ids, and a
`commit(freshId) → boolean` callback carrying the caller-specific metadata change. Its contract:
a seed throw is reported once through `reportPersistError(persistStorageWriteError(error))`, the
fresh id is rolled back, and the result is a refusal; a `false` commit rolls back the fresh id
(`removeTreeSafely`, else `recordFailedAdmission`, as `rollbackCreatedTree` does today). **A thrown
commit keeps `commitNewTab`'s existing, test-pinned contract unchanged**
(`src/utils/tabs.test.ts:290-314`, "a throwing admission preserves a potentially committed tree
without a failed-admission marker"; `tabs.ts:93-95,107`): the outcome is uncertain because the
envelope may already hold the fresh id, so the staged tree is neither deleted nor marked, nothing
is reported by the helper, and the error propagates. If the envelope did not take the fresh id,
its tree is unreferenced and the existing startup sweep of authoritative workspaces reclaims it
(`workspace.ts:128-138,404-409`). `commitNewTab` and the new transaction both route through this
helper; the rollback stays in one place.

**Replace-new-tab transaction.** One exported function in `src/utils/tabs.ts`, taking the Jotai
store, the owner id, the tab fields and the tree, returning
`{ kind: "committed", id } | { kind: "superseded" } | { kind: "refused" }`. It uses only existing
atoms (`tabsAtom`, `activeTabAtom`, `closingTabsAtom`); it adds no atom. (`src/state/atoms.ts`
cannot import `@/utils/tabs` — `utils/files.ts` imports `@/state/atoms` — and `src/state/store/tree.ts`
reaches `@/state/atoms` through `utils/sound.ts`, so the function lives in `utils/tabs.ts`, which
may import both.) Required behaviour, in order, with no `await` inside:

1. **Owner check before any write.** If the workspace has no tab with the owner id, or that tab's
   type is not `"new"`, or the owner id is in `closingTabsAtom` (a close already in progress,
   `BoardsPage.tsx:105,118-146`), return `superseded` and write nothing.
2. **Stage and commit once** through the shared staged admission. The commit callback replaces
   the owner by `{...tab, value: freshId}` at the owner's index in one `tabsAtom` write, inside
   `startTransition` exactly as `commitNewTab` (`tabs.ts:82`) and tab close
   (`BoardsPage.tsx:144-146`) do, because the write can change `activeTab` after an `await`
   (the synchronous-selection crash fixed by `b829b562`).
3. **Selection neutral.** The replacement keeps the existing selection invariant of every in-place
   tab edit (`tabsAtom` keeps the current `activeTab` when it still exists, `atoms.ts:105-116`;
   the `NewTabHome` card handlers edit a tab without touching selection): the fresh id is active
   exactly when the owner is active at commit time, and no other selection change happens. This is
   identity preservation of the owner's position, not a new focus behaviour.
4. On `refused` (seed throw, or a refused save — already reported by `saveWorkspace`), the
   owner's metadata, durable tree, pending tree, cached tree store and atoms are untouched, and the
   fresh key is absent (or marked by `recordFailedAdmission`). **A thrown commit is not `refused`**:
   it propagates with an uncertain envelope. The reclaim in step 5 does not run, so the owner's
   durable tree, pending tree, cached store and atoms are still untouched, and the fresh tree is
   still present; whichever tab the envelope holds therefore references a tree that matches it
   (owner → owner tree, or fresh id → imported tree). If the envelope took the fresh id, the owner
   id's durable removal intent is in that same envelope.
5. **Reclaim on `committed`.** Reclaim the owner id through the same tab-local cleanup that
   `closeWorkspaceTabAtom` performs (`atoms.ts:144-159`: `removeFileFreshness`,
   `tabStorage.remove`, `disposeTabAtoms`, one reported cleanup error) — extracted into one
   exported helper in `src/state/atoms.ts` used by both — and then `closeTreeStore(ownerId)`,
   inside the same transition, as `BoardsPage.closeTab` does after `closeWorkspaceTab`
   (`BoardsPage.tsx:145-146`). The durable
   removal intent for the owner id is recorded by `commitWorkspaceAtom`'s closed-id reconciliation
   in the same envelope. The fresh id has no cached store, so `TreeStateProvider` hydrates it from
   the seeded durable value.

### O3 — Async ownership (depends on O1, O2)

* All three forms build their tree and tab fields, then make exactly one call of the O2
  transaction through one local helper in `ImportModal` (no per-branch copies).
* A result arriving after the owner was closed, is being closed, or was already replaced is
  `superseded`: nothing is written, no recent file is added, no error is shown.
* **Pending covers every active request.** `loading` is derived from a count of in-flight
  submissions (incremented before the first `await`, decremented when that submission settles),
  not from one boolean each submission clears on its own; with a single boolean, one settling
  request cleared `loading` while another could still land (round-2 evidence, I3 reopened).
  Later submits are not ignored; overlapping submissions resolve through `superseded`.
* While any import is in flight the modal cannot be dismissed: `ImportModal` passes
  `pending={loading}` to `AppModal`, whose shared contract already refuses close button, Escape and
  outside click for pending work (`AppModal.tsx:11-20`). A dismissed request therefore never lands
  silently in the owner later.

### O4 — Failure surfacing and retry (depends on O2, O3)

* `refused`: the persistence error is visible through the existing `reportPersistError`
  notification, once; the modal's own `submitError` line is not set for it. The owner is
  guaranteed unchanged (O2.4). If the owner's page is still mounted, the modal stays open with its
  inputs and `loading` cleared, and pressing Import again performs a complete new transaction. If
  the owner's page was unmounted meanwhile (the user switched tabs; `BoardsPage` uses
  `keepMounted={false}`), the notification is the only surface, and the user can import again
  from the unchanged owner tab.
* **Thrown** (O2 uncertain outcome — this is not `refused`): caught by `ImportModal`'s single
  transaction-call helper and reported once through the app-wide notification
  (`notifyUnlessCancelled(t("Common.Error"), error)`, as `NewTabHome` does), so it is visible even
  when the modal is unmounted; the helper does not rethrow it, so the outer `catch` sets no
  `submitError` for it. **No promise is made** that the owner is unchanged or that a retry path
  exists: the envelope may already hold the fresh tab. The only guarantee is O2.4's
  envelope/tree consistency; a later submit, if one is possible, is an ordinary new transaction
  computed from the live workspace, and any staged tree it leaves unreferenced is reclaimed by the
  startup sweep.
* `addRecentFileAtom` runs only after `committed`.

## Decided autonomously

1. **Fresh id instead of in-place replacement of the existing id.** Rejected: an existing-id
   transaction that snapshots and restores the owner's durable tree, pending entry and cached
   store — it needs restore-on-failure of three stores plus a window where the durable tree is
   already overwritten, which is exactly the defect class; the fresh-id protocol is already proven
   under `d-20260910-10`. Reason: the owner is always a `"new"` tab and the tab id is internal
   (users see the same tab position, name and content).
2. **Owner gone or closing → silently discard.** Closing the requesting tab is the user's cancel.
   Rejected: completing the import into a new tab (would resurrect a tab the user closed).

Both are technical calls under `d-20260910-10`; neither changes what a user is promised. Selection
behaviour is not decided here — it follows the existing in-place-edit invariant (O2.3).

## Risks / open questions

* `closeTreeStore(ownerId)` and the tab-local reclaim must run only on `committed`; running them
  on `refused` would drop state whose tab still exists.
* `NewTabHome` unmounts when the fresh tab replaces the owner; `handleSubmit` finishes on an
  unmounted component, so state updates after `committed` stay limited to what the existing code
  already does in `finally`.

## Not part of this task

* `InfoPanel.setPage` (metadata first, then live `setState`) and `FileFreshnessGate` append:
  existing-tab updates of file-backed tabs whose tree goes through the live store's persist path;
  their cross-key crash window is the limit `d-20260910-10` records, not this finding.
* The pasted-PGN branch (already creates a file and opens it through new-id creation).
* The save-to-collection copy: it is created before the tab transaction, as today; this plan does
  not change or specify its fate when the tab commit is refused or superseded.
* Cross-key process-crash atomicity (`d-20260910-10` limits).

## Phases

### Phase 1 — transaction primitive (O2)

Files: `src/utils/tabs.ts` (shared staged admission, `commitNewTab` routed through it, the
replace-new-tab transaction), `src/state/atoms.ts` (extracted tab-local reclaim helper used by
`closeWorkspaceTabAtom`), tests in `src/utils/tabs.test.ts` and `src/state/atoms.lifecycle.test.ts`.
Touches persistence and workspace ownership → `--role sensitive`.

Real-store tests (real Jotai store, real `tabStorage`, real `tree.ts`, jsdom `sessionStorage`).
Fixture: an owner `"new"` tab with a durable tree, a pending tree, and a cached store obtained
from `createTreeStore(ownerId)`; a second tab. Cases and required assertions:

* **Success**, owner active and owner inactive: the fresh id sits at the owner's index; it is
  active iff the owner was active, otherwise the prior selection is unchanged; the fresh durable
  value decodes to the imported tree; the owner's pending entry and durable key are gone; the
  owner id is in the workspace's pending-removal intent or its key is absent;
  `createTreeStore(ownerId)` no longer returns the old cached instance; a tab-scoped atom-family
  entry created for the owner before the call (e.g. `tabFamily(ownerId)`) is disposed; the second
  tab's metadata, tree and atoms are unchanged.
* **Tree write refused** (seed throws a `QuotaExceededError`) and **workspace envelope refused**:
  for each, the owner's metadata, workspace bytes, durable bytes and pending entry are identical to
  before; `createTreeStore(ownerId)` returns the same cached instance with the same `root` and
  `headers`; the owner's tab-scoped atom-family entry is the same instance; the fresh key is
  absent; exactly one persistence error is reported; a retry after the write is restored returns
  `committed`.
* **Thrown commit**, before and after the workspace write lands: the error propagates; the staged
  tree is neither deleted nor marked; no persistence report is emitted by the helper (spy on
  `reportPersistError`, also added to the existing `commitNewTab` throw case); the owner's durable tree, pending entry, cached store and
  atoms are untouched; after the write landed, `loadWorkspace` holds the fresh id with its tree and
  the owner id in the pending-removal intent; before it landed, `loadWorkspace` holds the owner and
  the fresh key is unreferenced. `commitNewTab`'s existing test `tabs.test.ts:290-314` stays green
  unchanged.
* **Superseded**: owner absent, owner type not `"new"`, owner id in `closingTabsAtom` — no key and
  no workspace byte written.
* **Reload**: `loadWorkspace` from the same `sessionStorage` after success shows the fresh tab with
  its tree; after refusal shows the unchanged owner.

PROOF: `pnpm exec vitest run src/utils/tabs.test.ts src/state/atoms.lifecycle.test.ts && pnpm exec tsgo --noEmit`

### Phase 2 — ImportModal routing (O1, O3, O4)

Files: `src/components/tabs/ImportModal.tsx`, `src/components/tabs/NewTabHome.tsx`,
`src/components/tabs/ImportModal.test.tsx`, `src/components/tabs/NewTabHome.test.tsx`. `--role normal`.

Tests: file, Link and FEN each call the O2 transaction once with the owner id prop **and the tree
that form produced** (file: the loaded tree with its `sourceStamp` and headers; Link: the parsed
PGN's headers; FEN: `headers.fen` equal to the normalised FEN) and the tab fields (name, type
`analysis`, origin); a result resolved after the transaction returns `superseded` adds no recent
file and shows no error; a `refused` result keeps the modal open with its inputs, sets no
`submitError`, adds no recent file, and a second press calls the transaction again; while loading,
the modal is rendered with `pending` while any of two overlapping deferred submissions is in
flight, and becomes dismissible only after both settle; a thrown transaction produces exactly one
notification and no `submitError` (mounted, and after unmount); a form never calls
`tabStorage.seed` (spy) and, with the transaction returning `refused`, the owner's durable key and
metadata are byte-identical. **Owner stability:** a deferred Link import whose active tab is switched to another tab (and,
separately, whose owner is replaced) before it resolves passes the original owner id — never the
now-active tab — to the transaction. `NewTabHome.test.tsx`'s `ImportModal` mock records its props,
and a test asserts `NewTabHome` passes its own `id` as the owner.

PROOF: `pnpm exec vitest run src/components/tabs/ImportModal.test.tsx src/components/tabs/NewTabHome.test.tsx && pnpm exec tsgo --noEmit && pnpm i18n:check`

### Phase 3 — container journey

File: `e2e/workspace-tabs.spec.ts` (reuses its `rejectWorkspaceWrites` / `restoreWorkspaceWrites`
helpers). FEN import from the New Tab page with workspace writes rejected → the tab stays the New
Tab page and the storage error is visible; restore → import succeeds and shows an analysis board
at that FEN (board visible); reload → still there. Two `capture`s — the refused state (New Tab
page with the storage error) and the imported board — each followed by
`await expect(page).toHaveScreenshot("<name>.png", { fullPage: true })` exactly as
`e2e/workspace-tabs.spec.ts:47-48` does (`capture` alone only writes to the run's output path,
`e2e/fixtures.ts:389-392`). The two new baselines are recorded inside the pinned container as
`.claude/skills/verify-ui/SKILL.md` and `d-20260919-13` allow, so each affected flow has a
committed snapshot comparison (`.claude/skills/push/SKILL.md:113-116`). `--role normal`.

PROOF: `pnpm test:e2e:container` (runs before the phase commit on a dirty tree; the final gate
`pnpm gate:ensure e2e-container` then runs on the clean committed tree — receipts are recorded only
for clean trees, `scripts/gate-receipt.mjs:305-306`).

### Final gates (per `.claude/skills/push/SKILL.md`, `src/**`)

`pnpm gates:contract:check` (includes `lint:ci`, `package.json:18`), `pnpm gate:ensure frontend-coverage`,
`pnpm gate:ensure frontend-mutation`, `pnpm gate:ensure frontend-build`, `pnpm bundle:check`, `pnpm gate:ensure e2e-container`.
Visible change is limited to the New Tab import journey. Under `.claude/skills/verify-ui/SKILL.md`
steps 5 and 7, the container suite (Phase 3) is the visual proof; `pnpm verify:app` is not
required because no IPC, lifecycle or process-teardown behaviour changes (renderer storage only),
and nothing depends on native GTK chrome.

may do to the staged tree and what it promises about the owner. r2 answer (rollback on throw) was
refuted by `tabs.test.ts:290-314`; r3 answer (marker + helper report) contradicts that same test
(`:312` asserts no marker) and double-reports through `runTabCreation`. Source trace: the pinned
contract preserves the staged tree without marker and propagates (`tabs.ts:93-95,107`,
`tabs.test.ts:290-314`); an unreferenced staged tree is reclaimed by the startup sweep of an
authoritative workspace (`workspace.ts:128-138,404-409`); a landed envelope carries the dropped
owner in its pending-removal intent (`atoms.ts:78-101`, `workspace.ts:397-405`). Judgment
(orchestrator, from the trace): keep the existing contract unchanged for both callers, separate
"thrown" from `refused`, and promise only envelope/tree consistency on a throw. Obligation changes:

### Round 4 — r4, 5 lenses (review-plan, correctness, error-handling, persisted-state, tests)

Root cause of the I5/I19 residue: the r4 edit inserted the new thrown-commit bullet into the
middle of the `refused` bullet, so the `refused`-only retry promise ("owner unchanged, import again
from it") read as a promise for a throw. This was an editing defect, not a design dispute; the
contract in O2 was accepted by every lens.

| ID | Witnesses | Claim | Disposition | Correction (r5) | MANDATE obligation |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|

### plan-r6.md

# Plan: f-20260910-09 — import replaces its owning New Tab through a fresh-id transaction

## Goal

Close `f-20260910-09`. Importing a game from the New Tab page (file, Link or FEN form of
`ImportModal`) must never write the tree of an existing tab id before its workspace metadata is
committed, must never overwrite a tab other than the one that asked, must keep the prior durable,
pending and live-cached state intact when any write is refused, and must be retryable.

MANDATE (fixed, verbatim from the ledger):

> importing a selected file into the current tab seeds the existing tab id before the workspace
> metadata is saved. A quota rejection of the workspace write leaves the prior durable origin/name
> referencing the replacement tree. A pending old tree also takes precedence over this seed in
> TabStorageRepository.read and can overwrite it on flush. The live cached store is not replaced
> by seed. … Open question: What shared existing-tab replacement transaction updates live store,
> pending tree, durable tree and metadata together while retaining the original game on failed
> commit and handling async import ownership? … the Link and FEN branches in ImportModal.tsx also
> seed the existing tab ID inside currentTabAtom updater callbacks, so the same existing-owner
> replacement design must cover all three import forms. Proof required: real-store tests with
> pending and cached old tree, successful replacement, quota failures on tree and workspace
> writes, stale async result, reload and retry; UI import journey through the container harness.

## Located facts (BASE 1854e32c)

* `src/components/tabs/ImportModal.tsx:112-124, 173-183, 193-205` — all three forms call
  `tabStorage.seed(prev.value, tree)` inside a `currentTabAtom` updater, then return new tab
  metadata. `seed` writes sessionStorage synchronously (`src/state/store/tabStorage.ts:458-474`)
  before `commitWorkspaceAtom` (`src/state/atoms.ts:76-103`) attempts the envelope write; a refused
  envelope leaves the old metadata over the new durable tree. `readTree` prefers `pending`
  (`tabStorage.ts:362-367`), and a cached zustand store (`src/state/store/tree.ts:197-201`, reused
  by `TreeStateProvider` and by `BoardsPage.closeTab` at `BoardsPage.tsx:106`) keeps the old state
  and rewrites it through `persist` on its next change.
* `ImportModal` is rendered only by `NewTabHome` (`NewTabHome.tsx:233`), which knows its own tab id
  (`id` prop, `BoardsPage.tsx:450`). `BoardsPage` renders panels with `keepMounted={false}`
  (`BoardsPage.tsx:268`), so after an `await` in `handleSubmit` the active tab — the one
  `currentTabAtom` resolves — can be any other tab the user switched to, including an analysis tab
  with a real game. The Enter handlers (`ImportModal.tsx:291, 303`) call `handleSubmit` even while
  `loading`, so two imports can race.
* `setCurrentTab`'s boolean result is ignored; `addRecentFileAtom` runs even when the commit was
  refused (`ImportModal.tsx:126-132`) — left unchanged by this plan (I29).
* The new-id protocol from `d-20260910-10` already exists: `commitNewTab` (`src/utils/tabs.ts:57-104`)
  seeds a fresh id, commits tabs and activation in one envelope write, and on seed failure or
  refusal rolls back only the fresh id (`rollbackCreatedTree`, `tabs.ts:106-111`, with
  `recordFailedAdmission` replayed at startup). It already replaces a lone `"new"` tab in place.
  `commitWorkspaceAtom` records every id dropped from `tabs` as a durable pending-removal intent
  (`atoms.ts:78-97`, `workspace.ts:142-160`). `closeWorkspaceTabAtom` (`atoms.ts:132-160`) then
  reclaims tab-local state: `removeFileFreshness`, `tabStorage.remove` (pending + durable),
  `disposeTabAtoms`; `BoardsPage` additionally calls `closeTreeStore` (`BoardsPage.tsx:146`).
* Import cycle constraint: `src/utils/files.ts` imports `@/state/atoms`, and `src/utils/tabs.ts`
  imports `./files`, so `src/state/atoms.ts` must not import `@/utils/tabs`.

## Approach

The answer to the open question is **not to replace an existing id at all**. The owning tab is a
`"new"` tab; the import becomes a fresh tab id that takes the owner's place in the tab list in the
same single envelope write. This is the `d-20260910-10` new-id protocol applied to an in-place
position, so the owner's durable tree, pending tree and cached store are never written before the
commit and are simply retained on any failure. Only after a successful commit is the owner id
reclaimed exactly as a closed tab is.

### O1 — Owner binding

`ImportModal` takes the owning tab id as a prop (`NewTabHome` passes its `id`), captures it when a
submit starts, and never resolves the target through `currentTabAtom`. `ImportModal` no longer
imports `currentTabAtom` or calls `tabStorage.seed`.

### O2 — Shared staged admission and the replace-new-tab transaction (depends on nothing)

**Shared staged admission (rule 11).** `commitNewTab` (`src/utils/tabs.ts:57-104`) already
allocates a fresh id, seeds it, commits, and rolls back. Extract that sequence into one helper in
`src/utils/tabs.ts` that takes the tree to seed (optional, as today), the existing ids, and a
`commit(freshId) → boolean` callback carrying the caller-specific metadata change. Its contract:
a seed throw is reported once through `reportPersistError(persistStorageWriteError(error))`, the
fresh id is rolled back, and the result is a refusal; a `false` commit rolls back the fresh id
(`removeTreeSafely`, else `recordFailedAdmission`, as `rollbackCreatedTree` does today). **A thrown
commit keeps `commitNewTab`'s existing, test-pinned contract unchanged**
(`src/utils/tabs.test.ts:290-314`, "a throwing admission preserves a potentially committed tree
without a failed-admission marker"; `tabs.ts:93-95,107`): the outcome is uncertain because the
envelope may already hold the fresh id, so the staged tree is neither deleted nor marked, nothing
is reported by the helper, and the error propagates. If the envelope did not take the fresh id,
its tree is unreferenced and the existing startup sweep reclaims it on the next load where that
sweep runs (authoritative or migrated workspaces, `workspace.ts:128-138,404-409`); a load with
uncertain tree ownership protects stored keys instead (`workspace.ts:282,404-406`), exactly as it
already does for `commitNewTab`'s thrown admissions today — no new retention behaviour. `commitNewTab` and the new transaction both route through this
helper; the rollback stays in one place.

**Replace-new-tab transaction.** One exported function in `src/utils/tabs.ts`, taking the Jotai
store, the owner id, the tab fields and the tree, returning
`{ kind: "committed", id } | { kind: "superseded" } | { kind: "refused" }`. It uses only existing
atoms (`tabsAtom`, `activeTabAtom`, `closingTabsAtom`); it adds no atom. (`src/state/atoms.ts`
cannot import `@/utils/tabs` — `utils/files.ts` imports `@/state/atoms` — and `src/state/store/tree.ts`
reaches `@/state/atoms` through `utils/sound.ts`, so the function lives in `utils/tabs.ts`, which
may import both.) Required behaviour, in order, with no `await` inside:

1. **Owner check before any write.** If the workspace has no tab with the owner id, or that tab's
   type is not `"new"`, or the owner id is in `closingTabsAtom` (a close already in progress,
   `BoardsPage.tsx:105,118-146`), return `superseded` and write nothing.
2. **Stage and commit once** through the shared staged admission. The commit callback replaces
   the owner by `{...tab, value: freshId}` at the owner's index in one `tabsAtom` write, inside
   `startTransition` exactly as `commitNewTab` (`tabs.ts:82`) and tab close
   (`BoardsPage.tsx:144-146`) do, because the write can change `activeTab` after an `await`
   (the synchronous-selection crash fixed by `b829b562`).
3. **Selection neutral.** The replacement keeps the existing selection invariant of every in-place
   tab edit (`tabsAtom` keeps the current `activeTab` when it still exists, `atoms.ts:105-116`;
   the `NewTabHome` card handlers edit a tab without touching selection): the fresh id is active
   exactly when the owner is active at commit time, and no other selection change happens. This is
   identity preservation of the owner's position, not a new focus behaviour.
4. On `refused` (seed throw, or a refused save — already reported by `saveWorkspace`), the
   owner's metadata, durable tree, pending tree, cached tree store and atoms are untouched, and the
   fresh key is absent (or marked by `recordFailedAdmission`). **A thrown commit is not `refused`**:
   it propagates with an uncertain envelope. The reclaim in step 5 does not run, so the owner's
   durable tree, pending tree, cached store and atoms are still untouched, and the fresh tree is
   still present; whichever tab the envelope holds therefore references a tree that matches it
   (owner → owner tree, or fresh id → imported tree). If the envelope took the fresh id, the owner
   id's durable removal intent is in that same envelope.
5. **Reclaim on `committed`.** Reclaim the owner id through the same tab-local cleanup that
   `closeWorkspaceTabAtom` performs (`atoms.ts:144-159`: `removeFileFreshness`,
   `tabStorage.remove`, `disposeTabAtoms`, one reported cleanup error) — extracted into one
   exported helper in `src/state/atoms.ts` used by both — and then `closeTreeStore(ownerId)`,
   inside the same transition, as `BoardsPage.closeTab` does after `closeWorkspaceTab`
   (`BoardsPage.tsx:145-146`). The durable
   removal intent for the owner id is recorded by `commitWorkspaceAtom`'s closed-id reconciliation
   in the same envelope. The fresh id has no cached store, so `TreeStateProvider` hydrates it from
   the seeded durable value.

### O3 — Async ownership (depends on O1, O2)

* All three forms build their tree and tab fields, then make exactly one call of the O2
  transaction through one local helper in `ImportModal` (no per-branch copies).
* A result arriving after the owner was closed, is being closed, or was already replaced is
  `superseded`: no tab or tree is written and no error is shown.
* **Pending covers every active request.** `loading` is derived from a count of in-flight
  submissions (incremented before the first `await`, decremented when that submission settles),
  not from one boolean each submission clears on its own; with a single boolean, one settling
  request cleared `loading` while another could still land (round-2 evidence, I3 reopened).
  Later submits are not ignored; overlapping submissions resolve through `superseded`.
* While any import is in flight the modal cannot be dismissed: `ImportModal` passes
  `pending={loading}` to `AppModal`, whose shared contract already refuses close button, Escape and
  outside click for pending work (`AppModal.tsx:11-20`). A dismissed request therefore never lands
  silently in the owner later.

### O4 — Failure surfacing and retry (depends on O2, O3)

* `refused`: the persistence error is visible through the existing `reportPersistError`
  notification, once; the modal's own `submitError` line is not set for it. The owner is
  guaranteed unchanged (O2.4). If the owner's page is still mounted, the modal stays open with its
  inputs and `loading` cleared, and pressing Import again performs a complete new transaction. If
  the owner's page was unmounted meanwhile (the user switched tabs; `BoardsPage` uses
  `keepMounted={false}`), the notification is the only surface, and the user can import again
  from the unchanged owner tab.
* **Thrown** (O2 uncertain outcome — this is not `refused`): caught by `ImportModal`'s single
  transaction-call helper and reported once through the app-wide notification
  (`notifyUnlessCancelled(t("Common.Error"), error)`, as `NewTabHome` does), so it is visible even
  when the modal is unmounted; the helper does not rethrow it, so the outer `catch` sets no
  `submitError` for it. **No promise is made** that the owner is unchanged or that a retry path
  exists: the envelope may already hold the fresh tab. The only guarantee is O2.4's
  envelope/tree consistency; a later submit, if one is possible, is an ordinary new transaction
  computed from the live workspace, and any staged tree it leaves unreferenced is subject to the
  existing startup sweep under the conditions stated in O2.
* The recent-file addition of the file form is unchanged: it still runs after the tab step,
  independent of the transaction's result (not part of MANDATE; round 5, I29).

## Decided autonomously

1. **Fresh id instead of in-place replacement of the existing id.** Rejected: an existing-id
   transaction that snapshots and restores the owner's durable tree, pending entry and cached
   store — it needs restore-on-failure of three stores plus a window where the durable tree is
   already overwritten, which is exactly the defect class; the fresh-id protocol is already proven
   under `d-20260910-10`. Reason: the owner is always a `"new"` tab and the tab id is internal
   (users see the same tab position, name and content).
2. **Owner gone or closing → silently discard.** Closing the requesting tab is the user's cancel.
   Rejected: completing the import into a new tab (would resurrect a tab the user closed).

Both are technical calls under `d-20260910-10`; neither changes what a user is promised. Selection
behaviour is not decided here — it follows the existing in-place-edit invariant (O2.3).

## Risks / open questions

* `closeTreeStore(ownerId)` and the tab-local reclaim must run only on `committed`; running them
  on `refused` would drop state whose tab still exists.
* `NewTabHome` unmounts when the fresh tab replaces the owner; `handleSubmit` finishes on an
  unmounted component, so state updates after `committed` stay limited to what the existing code
  already does in `finally`.

## Not part of this task

* `InfoPanel.setPage` (metadata first, then live `setState`) and `FileFreshnessGate` append:
  existing-tab updates of file-backed tabs whose tree goes through the live store's persist path;
  their cross-key crash window is the limit `d-20260910-10` records, not this finding.
* The pasted-PGN branch (already creates a file and opens it through new-id creation).
* The save-to-collection copy: it is created before the tab transaction, as today; this plan does
  not change or specify its fate when the tab commit is refused or superseded.
* Cross-key process-crash atomicity (`d-20260910-10` limits).

## Phases

### Phase 1 — transaction primitive (O2)

Files: `src/utils/tabs.ts` (shared staged admission, `commitNewTab` routed through it, the
replace-new-tab transaction), `src/state/atoms.ts` (extracted tab-local reclaim helper used by
`closeWorkspaceTabAtom`), tests in `src/utils/tabs.test.ts` and `src/state/atoms.lifecycle.test.ts`.
Touches persistence and workspace ownership → `--role sensitive`.

Real-store tests (real Jotai store, real `tabStorage`, real `tree.ts`, jsdom `sessionStorage`).
Fixture: an owner `"new"` tab with a durable tree, a pending tree, and a cached store obtained
from `createTreeStore(ownerId)`; a second tab. Cases and required assertions:

* **Success**, owner active and owner inactive: the fresh id sits at the owner's index; it is
  active iff the owner was active, otherwise the prior selection is unchanged; the fresh durable
  value decodes to the imported tree; the owner's pending entry and durable key are gone; the
  owner id is in the workspace's pending-removal intent or its key is absent;
  `createTreeStore(ownerId)` no longer returns the old cached instance; a tab-scoped atom-family
  entry created for the owner before the call (e.g. `tabFamily(ownerId)`) is disposed; the second
  tab's metadata, tree and atoms are unchanged.
* **Tree write refused** (seed throws a `QuotaExceededError`) and **workspace envelope refused**:
  for each, the owner's metadata, workspace bytes, durable bytes and pending entry are identical to
  before; `createTreeStore(ownerId)` returns the same cached instance with the same `root` and
  `headers`; the owner's tab-scoped atom-family entry is the same instance; the fresh key is
  absent; exactly one persistence error is reported; a retry after the write is restored returns
  `committed`.
* **Thrown commit**, before and after the workspace write lands: the error propagates; the staged
  tree is neither deleted nor marked; no persistence report is emitted by the helper (spy on
  `reportPersistError`, also added to the existing `commitNewTab` throw case); the owner's durable tree, pending entry, cached store and
  atoms are untouched; after the write landed, `loadWorkspace` holds the fresh id with its tree and
  the owner id in the pending-removal intent; before it landed, `loadWorkspace` holds the owner and
  the fresh key is unreferenced. `commitNewTab`'s existing test `tabs.test.ts:290-314` stays green
  unchanged.
* **Superseded**: owner absent, owner type not `"new"`, owner id in `closingTabsAtom` — no key and
  no workspace byte written.
* **Reload**: `loadWorkspace` from the same `sessionStorage` after success shows the fresh tab with
  its tree; after refusal shows the unchanged owner.

PROOF: `pnpm exec vitest run src/utils/tabs.test.ts src/state/atoms.lifecycle.test.ts && pnpm exec tsgo --noEmit`

### Phase 2 — ImportModal routing (O1, O3, O4)

Files: `src/components/tabs/ImportModal.tsx`, `src/components/tabs/NewTabHome.tsx`,
`src/components/tabs/ImportModal.test.tsx`, `src/components/tabs/NewTabHome.test.tsx`. `--role normal`.

Tests: file, Link and FEN each call the O2 transaction once with the owner id prop **and the tree
that form produced** (file: the loaded tree with its `sourceStamp` and headers; Link: the parsed
PGN's headers; FEN: `headers.fen` equal to the normalised FEN) and the tab fields (name, type
`analysis`, origin); a result resolved after the transaction returns `superseded` shows no error;
a `refused` result keeps the modal open with its inputs, sets no `submitError`, and a second press
calls the transaction again; while loading,
the modal is rendered with `pending` while any of two overlapping deferred submissions is in
flight, and becomes dismissible only after both settle; a thrown transaction produces exactly one
notification and no `submitError` (mounted, and after unmount); a form never calls
`tabStorage.seed` (spy) and, with the transaction returning `refused`, the owner's durable key and
metadata are byte-identical. **Owner stability:** a deferred Link import whose active tab is switched to another tab (and,
separately, whose owner is replaced) before it resolves passes the original owner id — never the
now-active tab — to the transaction. `NewTabHome.test.tsx`'s `ImportModal` mock records its props,
and a test asserts `NewTabHome` passes its own `id` as the owner.

PROOF: `pnpm exec vitest run src/components/tabs/ImportModal.test.tsx src/components/tabs/NewTabHome.test.tsx && pnpm exec tsgo --noEmit && pnpm i18n:check`

### Phase 3 — container journey

File: `e2e/workspace-tabs.spec.ts` (reuses its `rejectWorkspaceWrites` / `restoreWorkspaceWrites`
helpers). FEN import from the New Tab page with workspace writes rejected → the tab stays the New
Tab page and the storage error is visible; restore → import succeeds and shows an analysis board
at that FEN (board visible); reload → still there. Two `capture`s — the refused state (New Tab
page with the storage error) and the imported board — each followed by
`await expect(page).toHaveScreenshot("<name>.png", { fullPage: true })` exactly as
`e2e/workspace-tabs.spec.ts:47-48` does (`capture` alone only writes to the run's output path,
`e2e/fixtures.ts:389-392`). The two new baselines are recorded inside the pinned container as
`.claude/skills/verify-ui/SKILL.md` and `d-20260919-13` allow, so each affected flow has a
committed snapshot comparison (`.claude/skills/push/SKILL.md:113-116`). `--role normal`.

PROOF: `pnpm test:e2e:container` (runs before the phase commit on a dirty tree; the final gate
`pnpm gate:ensure e2e-container` then runs on the clean committed tree — receipts are recorded only
for clean trees, `scripts/gate-receipt.mjs:305-306`).

### Final gates (per `.claude/skills/push/SKILL.md`, `src/**`)

`pnpm gates:contract:check` (includes `lint:ci`, `package.json:18`), `pnpm gate:ensure frontend-coverage`,
`pnpm gate:ensure frontend-mutation`, `pnpm gate:ensure frontend-build`, `pnpm bundle:check`, `pnpm gate:ensure e2e-container`.
Visible change is limited to the New Tab import journey. Under `.claude/skills/verify-ui/SKILL.md`
steps 5 and 7, the container suite (Phase 3) is the visual proof; `pnpm verify:app` is not
required because no IPC, lifecycle or process-teardown behaviour changes (renderer storage only),
and nothing depends on native GTK chrome.

**Recurring dispute (rule 12a) — I5, thrown commit.** Contested invariant: what a thrown commit
may do to the staged tree and what it promises about the owner. r2 answer (rollback on throw) was
refuted by `tabs.test.ts:290-314`; r3 answer (marker + helper report) contradicts that same test
(`:312` asserts no marker) and double-reports through `runTabCreation`. Source trace: the pinned
contract preserves the staged tree without marker and propagates (`tabs.ts:93-95,107`,
`tabs.test.ts:290-314`); an unreferenced staged tree is reclaimed by the startup sweep of an
authoritative workspace (`workspace.ts:128-138,404-409`); a landed envelope carries the dropped
owner in its pending-removal intent (`atoms.ts:78-101`, `workspace.ts:397-405`). Judgment
(orchestrator, from the trace): keep the existing contract unchanged for both callers, separate
"thrown" from `refused`, and promise only envelope/tree consistency on a throw. Obligation changes:
O2 helper contract, O2.4, O4 thrown bullet, Phase 1 thrown case, Phase 2 thrown case.

### plan-r7.md

# Plan: f-20260910-09 — import replaces its owning New Tab through a fresh-id transaction

## Goal

Close `f-20260910-09`. Importing a game from the New Tab page (file, Link or FEN form of
`ImportModal`) must never write the tree of an existing tab id before its workspace metadata is
committed, must never overwrite a tab other than the one that asked, must keep the prior durable,
pending and live-cached state intact when any write is refused, and must be retryable.

MANDATE (fixed, verbatim from the ledger):

> importing a selected file into the current tab seeds the existing tab id before the workspace
> metadata is saved. A quota rejection of the workspace write leaves the prior durable origin/name
> referencing the replacement tree. A pending old tree also takes precedence over this seed in
> TabStorageRepository.read and can overwrite it on flush. The live cached store is not replaced
> by seed. … Open question: What shared existing-tab replacement transaction updates live store,
> pending tree, durable tree and metadata together while retaining the original game on failed
> commit and handling async import ownership? … the Link and FEN branches in ImportModal.tsx also
> seed the existing tab ID inside currentTabAtom updater callbacks, so the same existing-owner
> replacement design must cover all three import forms. Proof required: real-store tests with
> pending and cached old tree, successful replacement, quota failures on tree and workspace
> writes, stale async result, reload and retry; UI import journey through the container harness.

## Located facts (BASE 1854e32c)

* `src/components/tabs/ImportModal.tsx:112-124, 173-183, 193-205` — all three forms call
  `tabStorage.seed(prev.value, tree)` inside a `currentTabAtom` updater, then return new tab
  metadata. `seed` writes sessionStorage synchronously (`src/state/store/tabStorage.ts:458-474`)
  before `commitWorkspaceAtom` (`src/state/atoms.ts:76-103`) attempts the envelope write; a refused
  envelope leaves the old metadata over the new durable tree. `readTree` prefers `pending`
  (`tabStorage.ts:362-367`), and a cached zustand store (`src/state/store/tree.ts:197-201`, reused
  by `TreeStateProvider` and by `BoardsPage.closeTab` at `BoardsPage.tsx:106`) keeps the old state
  and rewrites it through `persist` on its next change.
* `ImportModal` is rendered only by `NewTabHome` (`NewTabHome.tsx:233`), which knows its own tab id
  (`id` prop, `BoardsPage.tsx:450`). `BoardsPage` renders panels with `keepMounted={false}`
  (`BoardsPage.tsx:268`), so after an `await` in `handleSubmit` the active tab — the one
  `currentTabAtom` resolves — can be any other tab the user switched to, including an analysis tab
  with a real game. The Enter handlers (`ImportModal.tsx:291, 303`) call `handleSubmit` even while
  `loading`, so two imports can race.
* `setCurrentTab`'s boolean result is ignored; `addRecentFileAtom` runs even when the commit was
  refused (`ImportModal.tsx:126-132`), but not after a seed throw (`:113,207`); O4 aligns it with
  `openFile` (I30).
* The new-id protocol from `d-20260910-10` already exists: `commitNewTab` (`src/utils/tabs.ts:57-104`)
  seeds a fresh id, commits tabs and activation in one envelope write, and on seed failure or
  refusal rolls back only the fresh id (`rollbackCreatedTree`, `tabs.ts:106-111`, with
  `recordFailedAdmission` replayed at startup). It already replaces a lone `"new"` tab in place.
  `commitWorkspaceAtom` records every id dropped from `tabs` as a durable pending-removal intent
  (`atoms.ts:78-97`, `workspace.ts:142-160`). `closeWorkspaceTabAtom` (`atoms.ts:132-160`) then
  reclaims tab-local state: `removeFileFreshness`, `tabStorage.remove` (pending + durable),
  `disposeTabAtoms`; `BoardsPage` additionally calls `closeTreeStore` (`BoardsPage.tsx:146`).
* Import cycle constraint: `src/utils/files.ts` imports `@/state/atoms`, and `src/utils/tabs.ts`
  imports `./files`, so `src/state/atoms.ts` must not import `@/utils/tabs`.

## Approach

The answer to the open question is **not to replace an existing id at all**. The owning tab is a
`"new"` tab; the import becomes a fresh tab id that takes the owner's place in the tab list in the
same single envelope write. This is the `d-20260910-10` new-id protocol applied to an in-place
position, so the owner's durable tree, pending tree and cached store are never written before the
commit and are simply retained on any failure. Only after a successful commit is the owner id
reclaimed exactly as a closed tab is.

### O1 — Owner binding

`ImportModal` takes the owning tab id as a prop (`NewTabHome` passes its `id`), captures it when a
submit starts, and never resolves the target through `currentTabAtom`. `ImportModal` no longer
imports `currentTabAtom` or calls `tabStorage.seed`.

### O2 — Shared staged admission and the replace-new-tab transaction (depends on nothing)

**Shared staged admission (rule 11).** `commitNewTab` (`src/utils/tabs.ts:57-104`) already
allocates a fresh id, seeds it, commits, and rolls back. Extract that sequence into one helper in
`src/utils/tabs.ts` that takes the tree to seed (optional, as today), the existing ids, and a
`commit(freshId) → boolean` callback carrying the caller-specific metadata change. Its contract:
a seed throw is reported once through `reportPersistError(persistStorageWriteError(error))`, the
fresh id is rolled back, and the result is a refusal; a `false` commit rolls back the fresh id
(`removeTreeSafely`, else `recordFailedAdmission`, as `rollbackCreatedTree` does today). **A thrown
commit keeps `commitNewTab`'s existing, test-pinned contract unchanged**
(`src/utils/tabs.test.ts:290-314`, "a throwing admission preserves a potentially committed tree
without a failed-admission marker"; `tabs.ts:93-95,107`): the outcome is uncertain because the
envelope may already hold the fresh id, so the staged tree is neither deleted nor marked, nothing
is reported by the helper, and the error propagates. If the envelope did not take the fresh id,
its tree is unreferenced and the existing startup sweep reclaims it on the next load where that
sweep runs (authoritative or migrated workspaces, `workspace.ts:128-138,404-409`); a load with
uncertain tree ownership protects stored keys instead (`workspace.ts:282,404-406`), exactly as it
already does for `commitNewTab`'s thrown admissions today — no new retention behaviour. `commitNewTab` and the new transaction both route through this
helper; the rollback stays in one place.

**Replace-new-tab transaction.** One exported function in `src/utils/tabs.ts`, taking the Jotai
store, the owner id, the tab fields and the tree, returning
`{ kind: "committed", id } | { kind: "superseded" } | { kind: "refused" }`. It uses only existing
atoms (`tabsAtom`, `activeTabAtom`, `closingTabsAtom`); it adds no atom. (`src/state/atoms.ts`
cannot import `@/utils/tabs` — `utils/files.ts` imports `@/state/atoms` — and `src/state/store/tree.ts`
reaches `@/state/atoms` through `utils/sound.ts`, so the function lives in `utils/tabs.ts`, which
may import both.) Required behaviour, in order, with no `await` inside:

1. **Owner check before any write.** If the workspace has no tab with the owner id, or that tab's
   type is not `"new"`, or the owner id is in `closingTabsAtom` (a close already in progress,
   `BoardsPage.tsx:105,118-146`), return `superseded` and write nothing.
2. **Stage and commit once** through the shared staged admission. The commit callback replaces
   the owner by `{...tab, value: freshId}` at the owner's index in one `tabsAtom` write, inside
   `startTransition` exactly as `commitNewTab` (`tabs.ts:82`) and tab close
   (`BoardsPage.tsx:144-146`) do, because the write can change `activeTab` after an `await`
   (the synchronous-selection crash fixed by `b829b562`).
3. **Selection neutral.** The replacement keeps the existing selection invariant of every in-place
   tab edit (`tabsAtom` keeps the current `activeTab` when it still exists, `atoms.ts:105-116`;
   the `NewTabHome` card handlers edit a tab without touching selection): the fresh id is active
   exactly when the owner is active at commit time, and no other selection change happens. This is
   identity preservation of the owner's position, not a new focus behaviour.
4. On `refused` (seed throw, or a refused save — already reported by `saveWorkspace`), the
   owner's metadata, durable tree, pending tree, cached tree store and atoms are untouched, and the
   fresh key is absent (or marked by `recordFailedAdmission`). **A thrown commit is not `refused`**:
   it propagates with an uncertain envelope. The reclaim in step 5 does not run, so the owner's
   durable tree, pending tree, cached store and atoms are still untouched, and the fresh tree is
   still present; whichever tab the envelope holds therefore references a tree that matches it
   (owner → owner tree, or fresh id → imported tree). If the envelope took the fresh id, the owner
   id's durable removal intent is in that same envelope.
5. **Reclaim on `committed`.** Reclaim the owner id through the same tab-local cleanup that
   `closeWorkspaceTabAtom` performs (`atoms.ts:144-159`: `removeFileFreshness`,
   `tabStorage.remove`, `disposeTabAtoms`, one reported cleanup error) — extracted into one
   exported helper in `src/state/atoms.ts` used by both — and then `closeTreeStore(ownerId)`,
   inside the same transition, as `BoardsPage.closeTab` does after `closeWorkspaceTab`
   (`BoardsPage.tsx:145-146`). The durable
   removal intent for the owner id is recorded by `commitWorkspaceAtom`'s closed-id reconciliation
   in the same envelope. The fresh id has no cached store, so `TreeStateProvider` hydrates it from
   the seeded durable value.

### O3 — Async ownership (depends on O1, O2)

* All three forms build their tree and tab fields, then make exactly one call of the O2
  transaction through one local helper in `ImportModal` (no per-branch copies).
* A result arriving after the owner was closed, is being closed, or was already replaced is
  `superseded`: no tab or tree is written and no error is shown.
* **Pending covers every active request.** `loading` is derived from a count of in-flight
  submissions (incremented before the first `await`, decremented when that submission settles),
  not from one boolean each submission clears on its own; with a single boolean, one settling
  request cleared `loading` while another could still land (round-2 evidence, I3 reopened).
  Later submits are not ignored; overlapping submissions resolve through `superseded`.
* While any import is in flight the modal cannot be dismissed: `ImportModal` passes
  `pending={loading}` to `AppModal`, whose shared contract already refuses close button, Escape and
  outside click for pending work (`AppModal.tsx:11-20`). A dismissed request therefore never lands
  silently in the owner later.

### O4 — Failure surfacing and retry (depends on O2, O3)

* `refused`: the persistence error is visible through the existing `reportPersistError`
  notification, once; the modal's own `submitError` line is not set for it. The owner is
  guaranteed unchanged (O2.4). If the owner's page is still mounted, the modal stays open with its
  inputs and `loading` cleared, and pressing Import again performs a complete new transaction. If
  the owner's page was unmounted meanwhile (the user switched tabs; `BoardsPage` uses
  `keepMounted={false}`), the notification is the only surface, and the user can import again
  from the unchanged owner tab.
* **Thrown** (O2 uncertain outcome — this is not `refused`): caught by `ImportModal`'s single
  transaction-call helper and reported once through the app-wide notification
  (`notifyUnlessCancelled(t("Common.Error"), error)`, as `NewTabHome` does), so it is visible even
  when the modal is unmounted; the helper does not rethrow it, so the outer `catch` sets no
  `submitError` for it. **No promise is made** that the owner is unchanged or that a retry path
  exists: the envelope may already hold the fresh tab. The only guarantee is O2.4's
  envelope/tree consistency; a later submit, if one is possible, is an ordinary new transaction
  computed from the live workspace, and any staged tree it leaves unreferenced is subject to the
  existing startup sweep under the conditions stated in O2.
* The file form adds its recent-file entry only after `committed`, the same ordering as the app's
  only other recent-file writer, `openFile` (`src/utils/files.ts:139,152`: return on refused
  admission, add the recent file after it). Today's rule cannot be preserved literally: it adds
  the entry after a workspace refusal but not after a tree-seed throw (`ImportModal.tsx:113,
  126-132,207`), a distinction between two internal writes that the transaction replaces with one
  `refused` result (rounds 5–6, I29/I30).

## Decided autonomously

1. **Fresh id instead of in-place replacement of the existing id.** Rejected: an existing-id
   transaction that snapshots and restores the owner's durable tree, pending entry and cached
   store — it needs restore-on-failure of three stores plus a window where the durable tree is
   already overwritten, which is exactly the defect class; the fresh-id protocol is already proven
   under `d-20260910-10`. Reason: the owner is always a `"new"` tab and the tab id is internal
   (users see the same tab position, name and content).
2. **Owner gone or closing → silently discard.** Closing the requesting tab is the user's cancel.
   Rejected: completing the import into a new tab (would resurrect a tab the user closed).

Both are technical calls under `d-20260910-10`; neither changes what a user is promised. Selection
behaviour is not decided here — it follows the existing in-place-edit invariant (O2.3).

## Risks / open questions

* `closeTreeStore(ownerId)` and the tab-local reclaim must run only on `committed`; running them
  on `refused` would drop state whose tab still exists.
* `NewTabHome` unmounts when the fresh tab replaces the owner; `handleSubmit` finishes on an
  unmounted component, so state updates after `committed` stay limited to what the existing code
  already does in `finally`.

## Not part of this task

* `InfoPanel.setPage` (metadata first, then live `setState`) and `FileFreshnessGate` append:
  existing-tab updates of file-backed tabs whose tree goes through the live store's persist path;
  their cross-key crash window is the limit `d-20260910-10` records, not this finding.
* The pasted-PGN branch (already creates a file and opens it through new-id creation).
* The save-to-collection copy: it is created before the tab transaction, as today; this plan does
  not change or specify its fate when the tab commit is refused or superseded.
* Cross-key process-crash atomicity (`d-20260910-10` limits).

## Phases

### Phase 1 — transaction primitive (O2)

Files: `src/utils/tabs.ts` (shared staged admission, `commitNewTab` routed through it, the
replace-new-tab transaction), `src/state/atoms.ts` (extracted tab-local reclaim helper used by
`closeWorkspaceTabAtom`), tests in `src/utils/tabs.test.ts` and `src/state/atoms.lifecycle.test.ts`.
Touches persistence and workspace ownership → `--role sensitive`.

Real-store tests (real Jotai store, real `tabStorage`, real `tree.ts`, jsdom `sessionStorage`).
Fixture: an owner `"new"` tab with a durable tree, a pending tree, and a cached store obtained
from `createTreeStore(ownerId)`; a second tab. Cases and required assertions:

* **Success**, owner active and owner inactive: the fresh id sits at the owner's index; it is
  active iff the owner was active, otherwise the prior selection is unchanged; the fresh durable
  value decodes to the imported tree; the owner's pending entry and durable key are gone; the
  owner id is in the workspace's pending-removal intent or its key is absent;
  `createTreeStore(ownerId)` no longer returns the old cached instance; a tab-scoped atom-family
  entry created for the owner before the call (e.g. `tabFamily(ownerId)`) is disposed; the second
  tab's metadata, tree and atoms are unchanged.
* **Tree write refused** (seed throws a `QuotaExceededError`) and **workspace envelope refused**:
  for each, the owner's metadata, workspace bytes, durable bytes and pending entry are identical to
  before; `createTreeStore(ownerId)` returns the same cached instance with the same `root` and
  `headers`; the owner's tab-scoped atom-family entry is the same instance; the fresh key is
  absent; exactly one persistence error is reported; a retry after the write is restored returns
  `committed`.
* **Thrown commit**, before and after the workspace write lands: the error propagates; the staged
  tree is neither deleted nor marked; no persistence report is emitted by the helper (spy on
  `reportPersistError`, also added to the existing `commitNewTab` throw case); the owner's durable tree, pending entry, cached store and
  atoms are untouched; after the write landed, `loadWorkspace` holds the fresh id with its tree and
  the owner id in the pending-removal intent; before it landed, `loadWorkspace` holds the owner and
  the fresh key is unreferenced. `commitNewTab`'s existing test `tabs.test.ts:290-314` stays green
  unchanged.
* **Superseded**: owner absent, owner type not `"new"`, owner id in `closingTabsAtom` — no key and
  no workspace byte written.
* **Reload**: `loadWorkspace` from the same `sessionStorage` after success shows the fresh tab with
  its tree; after refusal shows the unchanged owner.

PROOF: `pnpm exec vitest run src/utils/tabs.test.ts src/state/atoms.lifecycle.test.ts && pnpm exec tsgo --noEmit`

### Phase 2 — ImportModal routing (O1, O3, O4)

Files: `src/components/tabs/ImportModal.tsx`, `src/components/tabs/NewTabHome.tsx`,
`src/components/tabs/ImportModal.test.tsx`, `src/components/tabs/NewTabHome.test.tsx`. `--role normal`.

Tests: file, Link and FEN each call the O2 transaction once with the owner id prop **and the tree
that form produced** (file: the loaded tree with its `sourceStamp` and headers; Link: the parsed
PGN's headers; FEN: `headers.fen` equal to the normalised FEN) and the tab fields (name, type
`analysis`, origin); a result resolved after the transaction returns `superseded` shows no error;
a `refused` result keeps the modal open with its inputs, sets no `submitError`, adds no recent
file, and a second press calls the transaction again; `committed` adds the recent file once;
`superseded` adds none; while loading,
the modal is rendered with `pending` while any of two overlapping deferred submissions is in
flight, and becomes dismissible only after both settle; a thrown transaction produces exactly one
notification and no `submitError` (mounted, and after unmount); a form never calls
`tabStorage.seed` (spy) and, with the transaction returning `refused`, the owner's durable key and
metadata are byte-identical. **Owner stability:** a deferred Link import whose active tab is switched to another tab (and,
separately, whose owner is replaced) before it resolves passes the original owner id — never the
now-active tab — to the transaction. `NewTabHome.test.tsx`'s `ImportModal` mock records its props,
and a test asserts `NewTabHome` passes its own `id` as the owner.

PROOF: `pnpm exec vitest run src/components/tabs/ImportModal.test.tsx src/components/tabs/NewTabHome.test.tsx && pnpm exec tsgo --noEmit && pnpm i18n:check`

### Phase 3 — container journey

File: `e2e/workspace-tabs.spec.ts` (reuses its `rejectWorkspaceWrites` / `restoreWorkspaceWrites`
helpers). FEN import from the New Tab page with workspace writes rejected → the tab stays the New
Tab page and the storage error is visible; restore → import succeeds and shows an analysis board
at that FEN (board visible); reload → still there. Two `capture`s — the refused state (New Tab
page with the storage error) and the imported board — each followed by
`await expect(page).toHaveScreenshot("<name>.png", { fullPage: true })` exactly as
`e2e/workspace-tabs.spec.ts:47-48` does (`capture` alone only writes to the run's output path,
`e2e/fixtures.ts:389-392`). The two new baselines are recorded inside the pinned container as
`.claude/skills/verify-ui/SKILL.md` and `d-20260919-13` allow, so each affected flow has a
committed snapshot comparison (`.claude/skills/push/SKILL.md:113-116`). `--role normal`.

PROOF: `pnpm test:e2e:container` (runs before the phase commit on a dirty tree; the final gate
`pnpm gate:ensure e2e-container` then runs on the clean committed tree — receipts are recorded only
for clean trees, `scripts/gate-receipt.mjs:305-306`).

### Final gates (per `.claude/skills/push/SKILL.md`, `src/**`)

`pnpm gates:contract:check` (includes `lint:ci`, `package.json:18`), `pnpm gate:ensure frontend-coverage`,
`pnpm gate:ensure frontend-mutation`, `pnpm gate:ensure frontend-build`, `pnpm bundle:check`, `pnpm gate:ensure e2e-container`.
Visible change is limited to the New Tab import journey. Under `.claude/skills/verify-ui/SKILL.md`
steps 5 and 7, the container suite (Phase 3) is the visual proof; `pnpm verify:app` is not
required because no IPC, lifecycle or process-teardown behaviour changes (renderer storage only),
and nothing depends on native GTK chrome.

## Reviews

### Round 1 — r1, 11 lenses (all plan-capable), executor codex, role sensitive / review-plan

Raw verdicts: chess-semantics APPROVED, engine-protocol APPROVED, tauri-security APPROVED;
